People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I mean… maybe, but I haven’t really counted them that way. There have been times before where I felt low, but I can’t say for sure how many.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more separate periods in your life where you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like the day’s already kind of ruined before I’ve even started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling a lack of energy or fatigue almost every day, even after resting?\n\nPatient: Yes… pretty much. I sleep, but it doesn’t really make me feel rested. Even just getting through work and then making it through dinner at home feels… heavier than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, socializing, or activities you looked forward to before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I usually really love painting, and going to museums, just wandering around exhibits. Lately my paints just sit there untouched, and even the idea of going out to see something doesn’t really… do much for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, not just in the mornings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days lately. It’s not only the morning. I kind of wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day, and it… tends to hang around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like a significant decrease or increase?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I haven’t been eating much at work… sometimes I just pick at meals or forget lunch. And my skirts are fitting looser, though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty, maybe even blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I keep going over things in my head—family plans I missed, arguments or tense moments with my husband. It makes me feel like I’m… not doing well by anyone. As a wife, a mother, even at work. Like I’m letting people down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had similar periods of feeling down or losing interest in life before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, it’s happened before. The longest stretch I can really remember was… maybe about five months. I just felt low and tired most days, not really like myself.\n\nDoctor: How long ago was that previous period when you felt low and tired most days, and have you had any other similar episodes since then?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure exactly how long ago… it’s a bit hard to place. I think there have been other times I felt kind of low like that, but I haven’t really kept track.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way during the current stretch—has it been more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been more than two weeks. I can’t confidently say it’s been two or more times like that. I’ve had low stretches, but I haven’t kept track of whether they lasted that long or were that constant.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more separate times in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things almost every day for at least a couple of weeks, and these times were separated by at least a couple of months where you felt like yourself again?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I’ve had times where I felt pretty low, but I can’t say for sure that it was two separate episodes like that, with months in between.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and confirm if there were at least two separate times in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things almost every day for at least a couple of weeks, with at least a couple of months in between where you felt like yourself again?\n\nPatient: I really can’t confirm that. I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’ve just been really exhausted. Like, sleep doesn’t seem to fix it, and getting through a normal workday, then dinner at home, feels harder than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like the day’s already kind of ruined before I’ve even started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling a lack of energy or fatigue almost every day, even after resting?\n\nPatient: Yes… pretty much. I sleep, but it doesn’t really make me feel rested. Even just getting through work and then making it through dinner at home feels… heavier than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, socializing, or activities you looked forward to before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I usually really love painting, and going to museums, just wandering around exhibits. Lately my paints just sit there untouched, and even the idea of going out to see something doesn’t really… do much for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, not just in the mornings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days lately. It’s not only the morning. I kind of wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day, and it… tends to hang around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like a significant decrease or increase?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I haven’t been eating much at work… sometimes I just pick at meals or forget lunch. And my skirts are fitting looser, though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty, maybe even blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I keep going over things in my head—family plans I missed, arguments or tense moments with my husband. It makes me feel like I’m… not doing well by anyone. As a wife, a mother, even at work. Like I’m letting people down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had similar periods of feeling down or losing interest in life before, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, it’s happened before. The longest stretch I can really remember was… maybe about five months. I just felt low and tired most days, not really like myself.\n\nDoctor: How long ago was that previous period when you felt low and tired most days, and have you had any other similar episodes since then?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure exactly how long ago… it’s a bit hard to place. It wasn’t recent-recent, but I can’t give you a clear date. I think there have been other times I felt kind of low like that, but I haven’t really kept track.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way during the current stretch—has it been more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been more than two weeks. I’d say this whole shift—from feeling really driven and energized to feeling low like this—has been around a month.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one previous episode where you felt this way before the current one?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… maybe, but I haven’t really counted them that way. There have been times before where I felt low, but I can’t say for sure how many.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more separate periods in your life where you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I can’t confidently say it’s been two or more times like that. I’ve had low stretches, but I haven’t kept track of whether they lasted that long or were that constant.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two or more separate times in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things almost every day for at least a couple of weeks, and these times were separated by at least a couple of months where you felt like yourself again?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say that clearly. I’ve had low periods, but whether there were two separate ones like that, with a couple months of feeling normal in between… I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had two or more separate times when you felt down or lost interest in things almost every day, each time lasting for a couple of weeks or more, with at least a couple of months in between where you felt like yourself again?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I’ve tried to think it through, but I don’t have it mapped out clearly enough to say there were two separate times like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been at least two different times in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things almost every day, each time lasting for at least two weeks, and with at least two months in between where you felt like yourself again?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I still can’t say for sure. I’ve had low periods, but whether they were separate like that and lasted that long… I’m not really sure.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if you’ve had at least two different times in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things almost every day, each time lasting for at least two weeks, with at least two months in between where you felt like yourself again?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to separate it out that neatly.
